People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. I haven’t been trying to lose weight, but my jeans feel looser. My appetite’s been off too—some days I’ll realize I’ve only had coffee and a few bites of food.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I think it’s been about four months now. It started around the time my spouse and I began arguing a lot more often.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of not wanting to be alive or of harming yourself?\n\nPatient: No, I haven’t had thoughts like that. I’ve been stressed, but I don’t want to hurt myself or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah, on some days I do. It’s like my body is moving through wet cement. Even answering a simple question from my spouse can take me a weirdly long time.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I feel bad when things are tense at home, but I wouldn’t say I’ve been feeling guilty about anything in particular.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt this way before?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I’ve had rough patches before, but I don’t know if it was exactly like this.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience pleasure most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, most of the time lately. I’ve kind of stopped caring about the things that usually make me feel like myself—sketching, going to gallery openings, cooking, yoga. Even playing tennis with a friend just doesn’t sound appealing. It’s not that I hate those things now, I just don’t really feel much from them.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these feelings of tiredness and lack of interest are interfering with your daily life most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it is. I’ve fallen behind on my certificate coursework, and basic stuff like bills, meals, and chores has been piling up. I make plans to catch up, but I can’t seem to keep them organized or follow through. My spouse has had to take over more at home, and I’ve been pulling back from friends and art things I’d normally care about.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these feelings have been present every day, or are there some days when you feel somewhat better?\n\nPatient: It’s been pretty much every day, honestly. Maybe some days are a little less awful than others, but the low interest and heavy tired feeling has been going on for about three months now.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks where you felt better and your interest in activities returned?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I haven’t had a two-week stretch where I felt back to normal or interested in things again. It’s been this quieter low period for about three months now, with the low interest and that heavy fatigue pretty much sticking around.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
